Infection VectorsVector lây nhiễm

  • Documents:Tài liệu: a .docm (macro-enabled) runs a malicious VBA macro after "Enable Content"; DDE and embedded objects are related Office vectors — inspect with olevba before opening.một .docm (bật macro) chạy macro VBA độc hại sau khi "Enable Content"; DDE và đối tượng nhúng là vector Office liên quan — kiểm tra bằng olevba trước khi mở.
  • Drive-by: exploits the browser/plugins simply by visiting a malicious or compromised page — no obvious user action.khai thác trình duyệt/plugin chỉ bằng việc truy cập một trang độc hại hoặc bị xâm nhập — không cần hành động rõ ràng.
  • Delivery tricks:Mẹo phát tán: ISO/IMG containers bypass Mark-of-the-Web so extracted files skip SmartScreen/Protected View warnings; LOLBins (mshta, regsvr32 "Squiblydoo", rundll32) run code while appearing legitimate; password-protected ZIPs evade gateway AV (the body supplies the password).container ISO/IMG vượt Mark-of-the-Web nên file trích ra bỏ qua cảnh báo SmartScreen/Protected View; LOLBins (mshta, regsvr32 "Squiblydoo", rundll32) chạy mã trong khi trông hợp lệ; ZIP có mật khẩu né AV tại gateway (thân email cung cấp mật khẩu).

E11 — Malware Behaviours and Anti-Forensics

Steganography detection methods

  • File size anomalies: Compare expected size with observed size for the media type.
  • Visual / audio review: Look for distortion, artifacts, or suspicious appended data.
  • Metadata and strings: Use strings, exiftool, and file identification.
  • Embedded data carving: Use binwalk or foremost to identify appended or embedded files.
  • Specialised tools: StegDetect may help with older JPEG steganography techniques.
  • Encryption: protect C2 / payloads or ransomware files.bảo vệ C2 / payload hoặc file ransomware.
  • Steganography: hide data in images / media.ẩn dữ liệu trong ảnh / media.
  • Password-protected archives: evade scanners and stage data.vượt qua scanner và dàn dựng dữ liệu.
  • Obfuscation: encoded strings, packed executables, control-flow tricks.chuỗi mã hóa, executable đã đóng gói, thủ thuật luồng điều khiển.
  • Covert storage: NTFS ADS, slack space, registry blobs, hidden directories.NTFS ADS, slack space, blob registry, thư mục ẩn.
  • Covert communications: DNS, HTTPS, ICMP, social / cloud platforms.DNS, HTTPS, ICMP, nền tảng mạng xã hội / cloud.
  • Data erasure: wiping tools, log deletion, timestomping, secure delete.công cụ xóa, xóa log, timestomping, xóa an toàn.

Malware Encryption Techniques

  • Payload encryption: Malware encrypts its C2 traffic, dropped files, and embedded data to evade detection and complicate analysis
  • Common algorithms: XOR (simple, fast), AES (strong, common in commodity malware), RC4 (legacy), ChaCha20
  • Custom encryption: Malware authors implement weak custom ciphers — often breakable via frequency analysis or brute force
  • Ransomware: Hybrid encryption — RSA to protect symmetric key, AES to encrypt files. Without private key, files unrecoverable.
  • Config encryption: C2 IP / domain, mutex, campaign ID stored encrypted in binary — decrypt by locating key and applying algorithm (see F6)

Password Protection & Covert Communication Techniques

  • Password Protection: Malware delivered in password-protected archives (ZIP, RAR, 7z) to bypass email gateway AV. Password provided separately in email body or SMS.
  • Covert storage: NTFS ADS hides files in metadata streams — file.txt:payload.exe invisible to normal dir listing. Detect with dir /r.
  • Covert C2 communication: Legitimate protocol abuse (HTTP / S, DNS, SMTP), domain fronting, LOLBins for C2 (certutil, bitsadmin, mshta).
  • Covert recon: Passive OSINT, slow scanning below IDS thresholds, blending with legitimate admin traffic.
  • Covert exfiltration: Incremental transfers, DNS-encoded data, steganography, cloud storage APIs.

Anti-Analysis & Evasion Techniques

TechniqueDescriptionDetection
Sandbox detectionCheck for VM artifacts: registry keys, file paths, MAC prefix, low uptime, few filesLong sleep timers, check for CPUID VM leaves
Anti-debugIsDebuggerPresent, CheckRemoteDebuggerPresent, timing checks (RDTSC)NOP out checks in debugger, ScyllaHide plugin
Code obfuscationJunk instructions, opaque predicates, API hashingDynamic analysis, emulation
Encrypted payloadPayload decrypted at runtime to avoid static detectionMemory dump after decryption, breakpoint on VirtualAlloc
TimestompingModify file timestamps to blend inMACB timestamp analysis — Born after Modified = anomaly
NTFS ADSHide data / code in alternate data streamdir /r, streams.exe, forensic tools
SteganographyHide data in image / audio filesStatistical analysis of file entropy, steganalysis tools
Data Erasure ApplicationsTools that securely wipe files (SDelete, Eraser) — leave traces in $LogFile, USN journalCheck for known wiper tool artifacts, MFT anomalies
Covert C2 / Recon / ExfilUsing legitimate protocols (DNS, HTTP) and trusted services for covert opsCorrelate with D5 / D7 / D8 network indicators
Password ProtectionEncrypted archives, password-protected docs to hide payloadsDetect encrypted archive formats, high-entropy attachments

Malware Behaviours & Anti-ForensicsHành vi mã độc & chống điều tra

  • NTFS ADS: Alternate Data Streams attach hidden data to a file (file.txt:hidden.exe) ignored by normal directory listings — detect with dir /R or Streams.exe.Alternate Data Streams gắn dữ liệu ẩn vào một file (file.txt:hidden.exe) mà liệt kê thư mục thường bỏ qua — phát hiện bằng dir /R hoặc Streams.exe.
  • Timestomping: forging file MAC times to blend in; usually only $SI is altered, so compare against $FN.giả mạo thời gian MAC của file để hòa lẫn; thường chỉ $SI bị sửa, nên so với $FN.
  • Destruction & evasion:Phá hủy & né tránh: a wiper/secure-deletion tool overwrites data to defeat recovery; disabling Defender, clearing event logs (1102) and deleting Prefetch are defense-evasion/anti-forensic behaviours — the gaps they create are themselves indicators. Steganography hides data inside another file (image/audio), concealing its very existence.wiper/công cụ xóa an toàn ghi đè dữ liệu để vô hiệu khôi phục; tắt Defender, xóa event log (1102) và xóa Prefetch là hành vi né phòng thủ/chống điều tra — các khoảng trống chúng tạo ra bản thân là chỉ dấu. Steganography giấu dữ liệu trong file khác (ảnh/âm thanh), che chính sự tồn tại của nó.

E12 — Rootkit Identification

  • Rootkits hide files, processes, registry keys, drivers, or network connections.Rootkit ẩn file, tiến trình, khóa registry, driver hoặc kết nối mạng.
  • Hooking: user-mode API hooks, kernel SSDT hooks, inline patching, filter drivers.Hooking: hook API user-mode, hook SSDT kernel, vá inline, driver filter.
  • Detection: compare high-level API output with raw disk / memory views; look for hidden drivers, suspicious kernel modules, and discrepancies.Phát hiện: so sánh đầu ra API cấp cao với view đĩa / bộ nhớ thô; tìm driver ẩn, module kernel đáng ngờ và sự không khớp.
  • Tools / approach: memory analysis, driver enumeration, GMER-like checks, Autoruns, kernel module review.Công cụ / cách tiếp cận: phân tích bộ nhớ, liệt kê driver, kiểm tra kiểu GMER, Autoruns, xem xét module kernel.

Cross-View Detection — psscan vs pslist and Bootkits

  • psscan vs pslist discrepancy: pslist walks EPROCESS doubly-linked list (manipulable). psscan scans raw memory for EPROCESS structures. Process in psscan but missing from pslist = DKOM rootkit hiding it.
  • modules vs modscan: Same cross-view for kernel drivers — modscan finds hidden drivers absent from module list.
  • SSDT hook detection: Volatility ssdt plugin. SSDT entries pointing outside ntoskrnl.exe / win32k.sys address ranges = kernel hook by rootkit.
  • Bootkit: Infects MBR or VBR — loads before OS, patches kernel during boot. Detect: compare first 512 bytes of disk against known-good baseline. Secure Boot prevents unsigned pre-OS code.
  • Filter driver detection: fltMC filters lists active mini-filter drivers. Unknown filter not in signed driver database = rootkit candidate.

How to Identify Rootkits — Cross-View Detection

Rootkits subvert the OS to hide their presence. Detection compares what the OS reports (potentially manipulated) against raw memory / disk reads (harder to manipulate). Discrepancy = rootkit indicator.Rootkit phá hoại OS để ẩn sự hiện diện. Phát hiện so sánh những gì OS báo cáo (có thể bị thao túng) với đọc bộ nhớ / đĩa thô (khó thao túng hơn). Sự không khớp = chỉ báo rootkit.

What to CompareRaw / Trusted SourceOS / Untrusted SourceTool
Running processespsscan (scans raw memory for EPROCESS structures)pslist (walks EPROCESS linked list — manipulable by DKOM)Volatility: psscan vs pslist
Files on diskRaw MFT enumerationDirectory listing via OS API (filterable)MFTECmd, Autopsy raw volume parsing
Registry keysRaw hive file parsingLive registry via API (filterable)RegRipper, RECmd (offline parsing)
Network connectionsRaw socket structure scan in memorynetstat (uses OS API)Volatility: netscan
SSDT entriesKnown ntoskrnl.exe export addressesLive SSDT valuesVolatility: ssdt, GMER
Loaded driversRaw driver scan in memoryDevice Manager / sc queryVolatility: modules vs modscan

Rootkit Categories and Hooking Techniques

TypeHiding TechniqueDetection
User-mode rootkitIAT hooking (modifies Import Address Table), EAT hooking, inline hooking (overwrites first bytes of function with JMP)Compare live IAT / EAT vs module on disk; look for unexpected JMP at function start
Kernel-mode (SSDT patch)Replaces kernel function pointer in SSDT with pointer to malicious functionVolatility ssdt plugin — non-ntoskrnl / win32k pointer = hooked
DKOMUnlinks EPROCESS from ActiveProcessLinks doubly-linked list — process runs but pslist skips itpsscan finds orphaned EPROCESS — compare vs pslist discrepancy
Filter Driver RootkitMalicious mini-filter intercepts file system / network I / OfltMC filters, Volatility driverscan, check against signed driver list
BootkitInfects MBR or VBR — loads before OS, patches kernel in memory during bootMBR comparison vs known-good; Secure Boot enforcement

Key Detection Principle

🔍
Cross-view detection

Compare what the OS reports (potentially compromised view) against raw memory / disk reads. Discrepancies between psscan and pslist, or between directory listing and raw MFT enumeration, indicate rootkit hiding activity.

Rootkit IdentificationNhận diện rootkit

  • Cross-view detection:Phát hiện chéo góc nhìn: when forensic memory analysis sees a process that live OS tools (Task Manager, tasklist) hide, a rootkit is manipulating the view — via API hooking or DKOM (unlinking the EPROCESS).khi phân tích bộ nhớ forensic thấy một tiến trình mà công cụ OS live (Task Manager, tasklist) ẩn, một rootkit đang thao túng góc nhìn — qua hook API hoặc DKOM (gỡ liên kết EPROCESS).
  • User vs kernel mode:User vs kernel mode: user-mode rootkits hook APIs within processes; kernel-mode ones load a driver at ring 0 (DKOM, SSDT patching) to subvert the OS system-wide and resist user-space tools.rootkit user-mode hook API trong tiến trình; loại kernel-mode nạp driver ở ring 0 (DKOM, vá SSDT) để lật đổ OS toàn hệ thống và kháng công cụ user-space.
  • Bootkit: infects the boot process (MBR/VBR/bootloader) to load before the OS and its defences — detect via offline disk/firmware/Secure Boot checks rather than trusting the running OS.lây nhiễm quá trình boot (MBR/VBR/bootloader) để nạp trước OS và phòng thủ — phát hiện qua kiểm tra đĩa offline/firmware/Secure Boot thay vì tin OS đang chạy.

E13 — Live Malware Analysis

  • Identify open files with Process Explorer / handle.exe.Xác định file mở bằng Process Explorer / handle.exe.
  • Identify registry activity with Procmon and Autoruns.Xác định hoạt động registry bằng Procmon và Autoruns.
  • Identify network sockets with TCPView, netstat, Process Hacker, or EDR telemetry.Xác định socket mạng bằng TCPView, netstat, Process Hacker hoặc telemetry EDR.
  • Monitor process creation, DLL loads, file writes, service creation, and scheduled tasks.Giám sát tạo tiến trình, tải DLL, ghi file, tạo dịch vụ và scheduled task.
  • Use isolated lab or carefully controlled live response; collect volatile evidence before shutdown.Dùng lab cô lập hoặc live response có kiểm soát cẩn thận; thu thập bằng chứng dễ mất trước khi tắt máy.

Live Malware Analysis Command Examples

netstat -ano                                      # Map live network sockets to PIDs

handle.exe -a suspicious.exe                       # Show open files, registry keys, and handles

tcpview.exe                                        # GUI view of process-to-network activity

procmon.exe                                        # Monitor file, registry, process, and network events

reg query HKCU\\Software\\Microsoft\\Windows\\CurrentVersion\\Run   # Check common user Run-key persistence

Analysing a potentially malicious process on a live running system — without taking the system offline.

Sysinternals Suite — Key Tools

ToolWhat It ShowsWhat to Look For
Process ExplorerProcess tree, parent-child, DLLs, handles, signed / unsigned, VirusTotal integrationProcesses with no icon / description, unsigned DLLs, unusual parent (svchost under explorer.exe), processes with no corresponding file on disk
Process Monitor (Procmon)Real-time file system, registry, process, network events per processRegistry writes to Run keys, file drops to temp directories, network connections, WriteProcessMemory calls
AutorunsAll persistence: Run keys, services, scheduled tasks, browser extensions, LSA providersEntries with no publisher / description, entries pointing to temp / appdata, newly added entries
TCPViewLive TCP / UDP connections with process name and PIDConnections to unknown external IPs, listening on unusual ports, connections from system processes
HandleAll open handles (files, registry keys, mutexes, pipes) per processMutex names (malware family tracker), open handles to sensitive registry keys / files
ListdllsDLLs loaded per process with full pathsDLLs from temp / appdata, DLLs without path (memory-only = reflective injection)

Key Live Analysis Commands

netstat -ano              → all connections + listening ports + PID

tasklist /m               → processes + loaded modules (DLLs)

handle.exe -p [PID]       → open handles for specific process

listdlls.exe [PID]        → DLLs loaded by process

reg query HKLM\...\Run    → autorun entries



Correlate PID from netstat → process in tasklist / Process Explorer

Foreign address 0.0.0.0 = listening socket

Established + external IP = active C2 or data transfer

Identifying Malware Behaviour Live

  • Procmon filter: Filter by suspect process name + RegSetValue / WriteFile / TCP Connect operations
  • Memory-only process: No corresponding file on disk — fileless malware indicator
  • Self-deletion: Malware deletes own file after execution — DeleteFile on its own path shortly after launch
  • Mutex names: Extract via Handle tool — unique mutex = malware family identifier, cross-reference with threat intel

Live Malware Analysis ToolsCông cụ phân tích mã độc trực tiếp

In an isolated lab: Process Explorer (handles/DLLs/registry keys a process has open) and TCPView (live sockets) reveal what a running sample is touching in real time. Procmon records real-time file/registry/process events; Regshot diffs system state before vs after detonation to reveal persistence keys and dropped files; Autoruns enumerates persistence.trong lab cách ly: Process Explorer (handle/DLL/khóa registry một tiến trình đang mở) và TCPView (socket sống) lộ những gì mẫu đang chạm theo thời gian thực. Procmon ghi sự kiện file/registry/tiến trình real-time; Regshot so sánh trạng thái trước/sau khi kích nổ để lộ khóa persistence và file được thả; Autoruns liệt kê persistence.

E14 — Linux OS File Structures

ArtefactUse
/var/log/auth.log or secureSSH, sudo, su, authentication.
/var/log/syslog or messagesSystem / service events.
~/.bash_historyUser commands; may be missing or tampered.
/etc/passwd, /etc/shadowAccounts and password hashes.
/etc/sudoers, sudoers.dPrivilege configuration.
cron directories / crontabPersistence / scheduled tasks.
systemd service filesService persistence.
~/.ssh/authorized_keysSSH persistence.
Apache / Nginx logsWeb compromise evidence.
/tmp, /var/tmp, /dev/shmCommon malware staging locations.

auditd — Linux Audit Framework

  • What: Kernel-level auditing subsystem that logs security-relevant events (file access, syscalls, authentication, process execution).
  • Config: /etc/audit/auditd.conf and /etc/audit/rules.d/
  • Log location: /var/log/audit/audit.log
  • Key commands:
# Search audit logs by time range

ausearch -ts today -te now



# Summary report of audit events

aureport --summary



# Failed login attempts

aureport --auth --failed



# Executed programs

aureport --executable



# File access events

ausearch -f /etc/shadow -ts recent
  • Forensic value: Provides syscall-level evidence of file access, process execution, and privilege use; more reliable than bash_history which can be edited.
  • Caveat: Must be configured before the incident; default rules may not cover all relevant events.

journald — systemd Journal

  • What: Modern replacement for traditional syslog; stores structured logs in binary format.
  • Log location: /var/log/journal/ (persistent) or /run/log/journal/ (volatile).
  • Key commands:
# View all journal entries

journalctl



# Filter by service

journalctl -u sshd



# Filter by time

journalctl --since "2024-01-01" --until "2024-01-02"



# Filter by PID

journalctl _PID=1234



# Export for offline analysis

journalctl -o json > journal_export.json
  • Forensic value: Structured metadata (PID, UID, command, timestamp); tamper-evident (forward sealing); can store kernel messages, service logs, and authentication events in one place.
  • Caveat: Binary format requires journalctl or compatible tools; may not be available on older distributions.

Linux Persistence Mechanisms

MechanismLocation / MethodDetection
systemd service/etc/systemd/system/, /usr/lib/systemd/system/List: systemctl list-unit-files --type=service; check for recently modified service files.
systemd timer/etc/systemd/system/*.timerList: systemctl list-timers --all; check for unusual timers.
cron jobs/etc/crontab, /etc/cron.d/, /var/spool/cron/, user crontabsCheck all cron directories; compare against known-good baseline.
LD_PRELOAD/etc/ld.so.preload or environment variableCheck /etc/ld.so.preload for unexpected libraries; inspect environment variables of running processes.
PAM backdoorModified PAM modules in /lib/security/ or /lib/x86_64-linux-gnu / security/Compare PAM module hashes against package manager; check for recently modified .so files in PAM directories.
/etc/rc.localCommands executed at boot (legacy but still works on some systems)Check /etc/rc.local for unexpected entries.
SSH authorized_keys~/.ssh/authorized_keysCheck for unexpected public keys; compare against user provisioning records.
Shell profile~/.bashrc, ~/.bash_profile, /etc/profile.d/Check for reverse shell commands, environment variable manipulation, or script execution.
Kernel module/lib/modules/, loaded via insmod/modprobelsmod for loaded modules; compare against known-good; check dmesg for module load events.

Linux Analysis Commands

CommandWhat It ShowsIR Use
ps auxfAll processes as forest (parent-child tree)Identify unexpected parent-child: sshd spawning bash = backdoor
lsof -iAll open network connections with processFind C2 connections and listening backdoors
lsof -p [PID]All open files for specific processIdentify files a suspicious process has open
ss -tulnpListening sockets with process namesFind backdoors on unexpected ports
find / -perm -4000 -type fAll SUID executablesFind malicious SUID binaries planted for privilege escalation
find /tmp /dev/shm -executable -type fExecutables in world-writable temp dirsMalware staging area detection

/proc/ Filesystem — Live System ForensicsHệ thống tệp /proc/ — Pháp y hệ thống trực tiếp

The /proc/ pseudo-filesystem exposes kernel and process state as files. It is volatile — contents exist only while the system is running and are not stored on disk. Critical for live response; not available in a disk image.Hệ thống tệp giả /proc/ hiển thị trạng thái kernel và tiến trình dưới dạng tệp. Nó biến động — nội dung chỉ tồn tại khi hệ thống đang chạy và không được lưu trên đĩa. Quan trọng cho phản ứng trực tiếp; không có sẵn trong image đĩa.

PathĐường dẫnWhat it containsNội dungForensic useỨng dụng pháp y
/proc/[PID]/cmdlineFull command line used to launch the processDòng lệnh đầy đủ để khởi chạy tiến trìnhReveals arguments and flags — catches renamed or obfuscated binariesTiết lộ đối số và cờ — phát hiện binary đổi tên hoặc che giấu
/proc/[PID]/exeSymlink to the executable file on diskSymlink đến file thực thi trên đĩaPoints to original binary path; shows (deleted) if binary was removed after execution (fileless indicator)Trỏ đến đường dẫn binary gốc; hiển thị (deleted) nếu binary bị xóa sau khi chạy (chỉ báo fileless)
/proc/[PID]/fd/Open file descriptors (files, sockets, pipes)File descriptor đang mở (tệp, socket, pipe)Identify open network connections, log files, and staging files held by the processXác định kết nối mạng đang mở, tệp log và staging file mà tiến trình đang giữ
/proc/[PID]/mapsMemory map — all regions loaded into process address spaceBản đồ bộ nhớ — tất cả vùng được nạp vào không gian địa chỉ tiến trìnhDetect injected DLLs (.so) or shellcode regions not backed by a file on diskPhát hiện DLL (.so) hoặc vùng shellcode bị chèn không có tệp tương ứng trên đĩa
/proc/[PID]/net/tcpActive TCP connections for the processKết nối TCP đang hoạt động của tiến trìnhIdentify C2 connections; correlate with lsof -p and ss -tulnpXác định kết nối C2; đối chiếu với lsof -p và ss -tulnp
/proc/[PID]/environEnvironment variables at process launch timeBiến môi trường tại thời điểm khởi chạy tiến trìnhReveal LD_PRELOAD injections, unusual PATH, or credentials passed as env varsTiết lộ chèn LD_PRELOAD, PATH bất thường hoặc thông tin xác thực được truyền qua env var
/proc/net/tcpSystem-wide TCP connection table (hex format)Bảng kết nối TCP toàn hệ thống (định dạng hex)Rootkit check: compare against ss / netstat output — discrepancies indicate hidden connectionsKiểm tra rootkit: so sánh với output ss / netstat — sự khác biệt cho thấy kết nối bị ẩn
/proc/[PID]/statusProcess metadata: PID, PPID, UID, GID, stateMetadata tiến trình: PID, PPID, UID, GID, trạng tháiVerify parent-child relationships and effective UID (privilege escalation indicator)Xác minh mối quan hệ cha-con và UID hiệu lực (chỉ báo leo thang đặc quyền)
# Read full command line of PID 1234
cat /proc/1234/cmdline | tr '\0' ' '; echo

# Check if binary was deleted after execution (fileless malware indicator)
ls -la /proc/1234/exe

# List all open files for PID 1234
ls -la /proc/1234/fd/

# Check memory regions — look for rwx anonymous mappings (shellcode)
grep 'rwx' /proc/1234/maps | grep -v '\.so'

# Compare /proc/net/tcp against ss output (rootkit check)
cat /proc/net/tcp  # hex-encoded IPs/ports
ss -tulnp          # userspace view — rootkit may hide entries here but not in /proc

Linux File TimestampsTimestamps tệp Linux

Linux (ext4) tracks three timestamps per file. Understanding them is critical for timeline reconstruction and detecting timestamp tampering (timestomping).Linux (ext4) theo dõi ba timestamp trên mỗi tệp. Hiểu chúng rất quan trọng để tái tạo timeline và phát hiện giả mạo timestamp (timestomping).

TimestampTimestampUpdated whenCập nhật khi nàoForensic significanceÝ nghĩa pháp y
mtime (modify)(sửa đổi)File contents are writtenNội dung tệp được ghiWhen data last changed; most useful for detecting file creation / modification eventsKhi dữ liệu thay đổi lần cuối; hữu ích nhất để phát hiện sự kiện tạo / sửa đổi tệp
atime (access)(truy cập)File is read (unless noatime mount option)Tệp được đọc (trừ khi dùng tùy chọn mount noatime)When file was last accessed; often disabled on modern systems for performance — treat as unreliable indicatorKhi tệp được truy cập lần cuối; thường bị tắt trên hệ thống hiện đại vì hiệu suất — coi là chỉ báo không đáng tin
ctime (change)(thay đổi)Metadata changes: permissions, ownership, hard link count, mtime itselfThay đổi metadata: quyền, chủ sở hữu, số hard link, bản thân mtimeCannot be directly set by userspace touch — ctime always updates when mtime is changed; timestomping changes mtime but ctime reveals it was tampered withKhông thể đặt trực tiếp bằng touch userspace — ctime luôn cập nhật khi mtime thay đổi; timestomping thay đổi mtime nhưng ctime tiết lộ sự giả mạo
  • Timestomping detection: If mtime predates ctime by a significant gap, the mtime was likely manipulated (touch -t changes mtime and atime but always updates ctime to now).Phát hiện timestomping: Nếu mtime trước ctime một khoảng đáng kể, mtime có thể đã bị thao túng (touch -t thay đổi mtime và atime nhưng luôn cập nhật ctime về hiện tại).
  • Timeline tool: stat filename shows all three timestamps. Forensic timeline tools (log2timeline / plaso) correlate file system timestamps with log entries to reconstruct attack sequence.Công cụ timeline: stat filename hiển thị cả ba timestamp. Công cụ timeline pháp y (log2timeline / plaso) đối chiếu timestamp hệ thống tệp với mục log để tái tạo trình tự tấn công.
# Show all three timestamps for a file
stat /usr/local/bin/suspicious

# Find files modified in last 24 hours
find /etc /usr/bin /usr/sbin -newer /tmp/reference_file -ls 2>/dev/null

# Check mtime vs ctime discrepancy (timestomping indicator)
stat file | grep -E "Modify|Change"

Package Manager Audit LogsLog kiểm tra trình quản lý gói

Package manager logs record software installation, removal, and upgrades — a key source for establishing what software was installed and when, which can identify attacker-installed tools or backdoors.Log trình quản lý gói ghi lại việc cài đặt, gỡ bỏ và nâng cấp phần mềm — nguồn chính để xác định phần mềm nào được cài đặt và khi nào, có thể xác định công cụ hoặc backdoor do kẻ tấn công cài.

DistributionBản phân phốiPackage managerTrình quản lý góiLog / history locationVị trí log / lịch sử
Debian / Ubuntuapt / dpkg/var/log/dpkg.log, /var/log/apt/history.log
RHEL / CentOS 7yum/var/log/yum.log, yum history list
RHEL / CentOS 8+, Fedoradnf/var/log/dnf.log, dnf history list
Any (installed packages)Bất kỳ (gói đã cài)rpmrpm -qa --last (sorted by install time)(sắp xếp theo thời gian cài đặt)
# Debian/Ubuntu: show recent package installations
grep " install " /var/log/dpkg.log | tail -50

# RHEL/CentOS: show yum transaction history
yum history list | head -20
yum history info [transaction-id]

# RPM: list all packages sorted by installation date (newest first)
rpm -qa --queryformat '%{INSTALLTIME:date} %{NAME}-%{VERSION}\n' | sort -r | head -30

# Look for tools commonly used by attackers
rpm -qa | grep -iE 'nmap|netcat|nc|socat|masscan|metasploit'
dpkg -l | grep -iE 'nmap|netcat|nc|socat|masscan'
Exam tip — Linux forensic evidence chainMẹo thi — Chuỗi bằng chứng pháp y Linux

Typical Linux compromise timeline evidence: (1) /var/log/auth.log — initial access (SSH brute force or key); (2) /proc/[PID]/exe (deleted) — fileless dropper executed; (3) ctime newer than mtime — timestomping; (4) package manager log — attacker installed tools post-compromise; (5) crontab / systemd service — persistence mechanism. Correlate timestamps across all sources.Chuỗi bằng chứng timeline xâm phạm Linux điển hình: (1) /var/log/auth.log — truy cập ban đầu (SSH brute force hoặc key); (2) /proc/[PID]/exe (deleted) — dropper không cần tệp đã chạy; (3) ctime mới hơn mtime — timestomping; (4) log trình quản lý gói — kẻ tấn công cài công cụ sau khi xâm phạm; (5) crontab / dịch vụ systemd — cơ chế duy trì. Đối chiếu timestamp qua tất cả nguồn.

Linux Investigation Patterns

SSH Compromise Detection

auth.log: Failed password for admin from X.X.X.X (multiple)

  → Accepted publickey for attacker from X.X.X.X

    → authorized_keys file modified timestamp matches

= SSH brute force or key-based backdoor

Web Server Compromise

access.log: POST to /uploads/suspicious.php

  → New process: /usr/sbin/apache2 -k start → child

    → Outbound connection from www-data to external IP

= Webshell with reverse shell

Cron-Based Persistence

crontab -l shows unusual entry (e.g., curl to external URL)

  → File referenced in cron entry is recently created

    → /var/log/auth.log shows sudo access before cron modification

= Cron-based persistence

Rootkit Indicators

ps aux shows fewer processes than /proc/[pid] enumeration

  → lsof shows connections invisible to netstat

    → lsmod shows unexpected kernel module

= Possible user-mode or kernel-mode rootkit

Log Tampering

auth.log gap (missing entries for time window)

  → .bash_history deleted or truncated

    → /var/log/wtmp modified timestamp unusual

= Anti-forensics / log cleanup

Linux OS File Structures & ArtefactsCấu trúc tệp & artefact Linux

ArtefactArtefactUseCông dụng
/etc/passwd / /etc/shadowAccounts / hashed passwords — watch new accounts, UID 0 duplicatesTài khoản / hash mật khẩu — chú ý tài khoản mới, trùng UID 0
cron, systemd units, ~/.bashrc, ~/.ssh/authorized_keyscron, unit systemd, ~/.bashrc, ~/.ssh/authorized_keysPersistence (authorized_keys = passwordless SSH)Persistence (authorized_keys = SSH không mật khẩu)
auth.log / secureAuthentication, sudo, SSH login eventsSự kiện xác thực, sudo, đăng nhập SSH
wtmp / btmp / lastlogLogins / failed logins / last login + source IPĐăng nhập / thất bại / lần cuối + IP nguồn
SUID binaries (find / -perm -4000)Binary SUID (find / -perm -4000)Run as owner (often root) — privilege-escalation routeChạy dưới quyền chủ (thường root) — con đường leo thang

Attackers may tamper with these logs — corroborate with bash history, file timestamps and other sources.Kẻ tấn công có thể can thiệp các log này — đối chiếu với bash history, mốc thời gian file và nguồn khác.

Appendix F
Malware Analysis / Reverse Engineering
Functionality identification, crypto extraction, PE structures, hiding techniques, and behavioural analysis.Xác định chức năng, trích xuất mã hóa, cấu trúc PE, kỹ thuật ẩn náu và phân tích hành vi.

F2 — Functionality Identification

YARA rules for IR and malware triage

rule Suspicious_PowerShell_Dropper

{

    meta:

        author = "IR analyst"

        description = "Detects simple PowerShell download cradle strings"

    strings:

        $s1 = "powershell" nocase

        $s2 = "DownloadString" nocase

        $s3 = /http[s]?:\/\// nocase

        $mz = { 4D 5A }

    condition:

        any of ($s*) or $mz at 0

}
  • rule: Names the detection logic.
  • meta: Documentation; not used for matching.
  • strings: Text, regex, or hex byte patterns.
  • condition: Boolean logic deciding whether the rule matches.
  • Hashes: YARA can use hash module functions, but exact file hashes are usually better handled by hash databases or EDR / SIEM queries.
  • IR use case: Hunt for related files across collected evidence, triage malware families, and encode resilient string / hex indicators.

Cryptographic constants in binaries

AlgorithmSpecific Constants to Look ForBehavioural / Structural IndicatorsWhy It Matters
AESS-box starting 0x63, 0x7C, 0x77…; Rcon values16-byte block operations, AES-NI instructions, CryptoAPI / Bcrypt callsEncrypted config, payload, ransomware file encryption
RC4256-byte S-array initialisation patternKSA / PRGA loops, byte swaps, frequent XOROlder malware config, traffic obfuscation
DES / 3DESDES initial / final permutation tables8-byte blocks, legacy CryptoAPI usageLegacy recognition
RSALarge integer math, public exponent 65537Certificate / key blobs, CryptoAPI RSA importsKey exchange; protects symmetric keys, not bulk data
MD5Constants 0xd76aa478, 0xe8c7b756…Compression rounds, hash API importsIntegrity checks, victim IDs, API hashing
SHA-1Constants 0x67452301, 0xEFCDAB89, 0x98BADCFECompression roundsLegacy hash recognition
SHA-256K constants starting 0x428a2f98, 0x71374491…64-round compression scheduleCurrent evidence integrity standard
Base64Alphabet A-Z, a-z, 0-9, +, / with = paddingDecoded layer in payloads; encoding, not encryptionDecode before deeper analysis
  • Crypto algorithm recognition: constants, S-boxes, API calls, loops, block sizes, imports.Nhận diện thuật toán mã hóa: hằng số, S-box, lệnh gọi API, vòng lặp, kích thước khối, import.
  • Network send / receive loops: socket/connect/send/recv, WinINet / WinHTTP, URLDownloadToFile, libcurl.Vòng lặp gửi / nhận mạng: socket/connect/send/recv, WinINet / WinHTTP, URLDownloadToFile, libcurl.
  • Persistence: Run keys, services, scheduled tasks, startup folder, WMI, launch agents.Persistence: khóa Run, dịch vụ, scheduled task, thư mục startup, WMI, launch agent.
  • Infection vectors: email, documents, exploit, drive-by, USB, trojanised binaries.Vector lây nhiễm: email, tài liệu, exploit, drive-by, USB, binary bị trojanised.
  • Use static and dynamic evidence together; strings alone are not enough.Dùng bằng chứng tĩnh và động cùng nhau; strings đơn thuần là không đủ.

API Hashing Techniques

Malware uses API hashing to resolve Windows API functions at runtime without importing them by name, making static analysis harder.

AlgorithmHow it worksRecognition
djb2hash = hash * 33 + c; simple string hashCommon constant 5381; multiplication by 33.
FNV-1ahash = (hash XOR byte) * primeFNV prime (0x01000193 for 32-bit); XOR then multiply pattern.
MurmurHashHash with mixing operations, rotation, and finalisationMixing constants, bit rotation patterns, final XOR / fold.
ROR13Rotate right 13 bits, add each characterConstant 0x0D (13) in rotation; seen in Metasploit shellcode.
CRC32Cyclic redundancy check used as hashCRC32 polynomial constants; lookup table.

Detection approach: Look for loops that process strings character-by-character with hash accumulation; find the hash resolution loop (compare hash against table → call resolved function); identify the hash algorithm by constants.Tìm vòng lặp xử lý chuỗi từng ký tự với tích lũy hash; tìm vòng lặp phân giải hash (so sánh hash với bảng → gọi hàm đã phân giải); xác định thuật toán hash bằng hằng số.

Common Malicious Windows API Patterns

APIMalicious useATT&CK mapping
CreateRemoteThreadInject code into another processT1055 Process Injection
VirtualAllocEx + WriteProcessMemoryAllocate and write to remote process memoryT1055 Process Injection
NtUnmapViewOfSectionProcess hollowing — replace legitimate process memoryT1055.012 Process Hollowing
SetWindowsHookExInstall hooks for keylogging or DLL injectionT1056.001 Keylogging
AdjustTokenPrivilegesElevate process privileges (e.g., SeDebugPrivilege)T1134 Access Token Manipulation
OpenProcess + ReadProcessMemoryRead memory of another process (credential dumping)T1003 OS Credential Dumping
RegSetValueExWrite registry keys for persistenceT1547 Boot or Logon Autostart
CreateServiceInstall malicious service for persistenceT1543 Create or Modify System Process
URLDownloadToFile / WinHTTPDownload payload from remote serverT1105 Ingress Tool Transfer
InternetOpen / HttpSendRequestC2 communication via HTTPT1071 Application Layer Protocol
Exam tip: CPIA-level questions usually expect recognition of constants / API patterns and the purpose of crypto, not full manual implementation analysis.Câu hỏi cấp CPIA thường yêu cầu nhận diện hằng số / mẫu API và mục đích của mã hóa, không phải phân tích thực hiện thủ công đầy đủ.

Functionality Identification — imports, strings & constantsNhận diện chức năng — import, chuỗi & hằng số

At CPIA level you summarise capability from the import table, strings and recognisable constants — not full manual analysis.Ở cấp CPIA bạn tóm tắt năng lực từ bảng import, chuỗi và hằng số nhận ra được — không cần phân tích thủ công đầy đủ.

API / string clusterCụm API / chuỗiLikely capabilityNăng lực khả dĩ
WSAStartup, socket, connect, send/recvNetwork / C2 (recv → decode → dispatch loop)Mạng / C2 (vòng recv → decode → dispatch)
InternetOpenUrl, URLDownloadToFileDownloader / dropperDownloader / dropper
RegSetValueEx → ...\Run, schtasks, CreateServicePersistence
SetWindowsHookEx(WH_KEYBOARD_LL), GetAsyncKeyStateKeylogger
GetDC + BitBltScreen captureChụp màn hình
EnumProcesses, OpenProcess, ReadProcessMemoryCredential theft / injection targetingTrộm credential / chọn mục tiêu tiêm
CreateMutex (fixed name)Infection marker (avoid double-run) — also an IoCDấu lây nhiễm (tránh chạy kép) — cũng là IoC

Crypto constants reveal algorithms without analysing the maths:Hằng số crypto để lộ thuật toán mà không cần phân tích phép toán:

Constant / patternHằng / mẫuAlgorithmThuật toán
0x67452301, 0xEFCDAB89…MD5 / SHA-1 init
0x63,0x7C,0x77,0x7B… (S-box)AES (Rijndael)
0x9E3779B9 (delta)TEA / XTEA
Fill array 0..255, permute by keyĐiền mảng 0..255, hoán vị theo khóaRC4 KSA

Few strings + a tiny import table (just LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress) = packed/obfuscated with dynamic API resolution. A custom/shuffled Base64 alphabet is obfuscation. Checks for VM artefacts (registry/MAC/CPUID) then exit = sandbox evasion.Ít chuỗi + bảng import nhỏ (chỉ LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress) = đã pack/làm rối với phân giải API động. Bảng chữ Base64 tùy biến/xáo trộn là làm rối. Kiểm tra dấu hiệu VM (registry/MAC/CPUID) rồi thoát = né sandbox.

F6 — Cryptographic Techniques

  • Find key material in strings, config blobs, resources, registry, command-line args, memory, or network handshake.Tìm vật liệu khóa trong chuỗi, blob config, tài nguyên, registry, tham số dòng lệnh, bộ nhớ hoặc handshake mạng.
  • Common mistakes: hardcoded keys, reused IVs / nonces, weak XOR / RC4, static salts, predictable PRNG.Lỗi thường gặp: khóa nhúng cứng, IV / nonce tái sử dụng, XOR / RC4 yếu, salt tĩnh, PRNG có thể đoán được.
  • Extract encrypted config by identifying decrypt routine and input buffer; emulate or debug if needed.Trích xuất config mã hóa bằng cách xác định quy trình giải mã và bộ đệm đầu vào; mô phỏng hoặc debug nếu cần.
  • Implementation weaknesses may allow decoding C2 traffic or recovering ransomware keys in poor designs.Yếu điểm trong thực hiện có thể cho phép giải mã lưu lượng C2 hoặc phục hồi khóa ransomware trong thiết kế kém.

Implementation Weaknesses — Identification Table

WeaknessCode IndicatorExploit Approach
ECB modeAES called with no IV; ECB mode constant in callSame plaintext block → same ciphertext. Statistical analysis reveals plaintext patterns from ciphertext alone.
IV reuseIV hardcoded as \x00\x00...\x00 (16 zeros)Same IV every session → same keystream. XOR two ciphertexts → cancel keystream → recover XOR of plaintexts.
Hardcoded keyKey as global constant in .data or .rdataExtract from binary → decrypt ALL victims' traffic with single extracted key.
srand(time()) weak RNGsrand(time(NULL)) before keygen; CryptGenRandom absentKey predictable from infection timestamp. Brute-force small timestamp range → candidate keys.
Rolling XOR (short key)XOR loop with i % key_len for indexKasiski test finds key length → frequency analysis per position → recover key.

Encryption Key Material — Identification and Extraction

Key TypeHow to Find in BinaryExtraction Method
Hardcoded AES key (16/24/32 bytes)High-entropy byte array in .data or .rdata section. Entropy analysis: binwalk -E. 16/32 non-printable consecutive bytes.Find array in disassembly → extract bytes → use in CyberChef AES decrypt
XOR key (1-4 bytes)Short constant in XOR loop. Loop structure + XOR instruction + small immediate value.Single byte: brute-force all 256 values → check for readable ASCII. Tool: xortool
Derived key (PBKDF2)Password string + salt constant + CryptDeriveKey or EVP_BytesToKey importsExtract password + salt → run same derivation function
Config blob keyBlob decrypted early in execution (WinMain / DllMain). Set breakpoint after decryption → dump from memory.Dynamic analysis — memory dump after decryption routine runs
RSA public keyLarge byte array (~256 bytes for 2048-bit), DER / PEM header, or CryptImportKey callCan extract public key — cannot decrypt without attacker private key (ransomware scenario)

Key Material Identification

  • Hardcoded keys: look for high-entropy byte arrays in .data or .rdata sections (16/24/32 bytes for AES)Khóa nhúng cứng: tìm mảng byte entropy cao trong section .data hoặc .rdata (16/24/32 byte cho AES)
  • Key derivation: PBKDF2, bcrypt — look for password string + salt constantDẫn xuất khóa: PBKDF2, bcrypt — tìm chuỗi mật khẩu + hằng số salt
  • XOR keys: often short (1-4 bytes), applied in loop — easy to identify via loop + XOR instruction patternKhóa XOR: thường ngắn (1-4 byte), áp dụng trong vòng lặp — dễ xác định qua mẫu vòng lặp + lệnh XOR
  • Key stored in config block: look for encrypted / obfuscated config blob decrypted early in executionKhóa lưu trong khối config: tìm blob config mã hóa / làm rối được giải mã sớm trong quá trình thực thi

Cryptographic Techniques in MalwareKỹ thuật mật mã trong mã độc

  • Key extraction:Trích khóa: even obfuscated keys must be cleartext in memory at the moment of use — set a breakpoint just after key setup/decryption and read the key buffer (or registers) at runtime. Then dump all decrypted strings from process memory at once.khóa dù bị làm rối vẫn phải ở dạng rõ trong bộ nhớ lúc dùng — đặt breakpoint ngay sau khi thiết lập/giải mã khóa và đọc buffer khóa (hoặc thanh ghi) lúc chạy. Rồi dump toàn bộ chuỗi đã giải mã từ bộ nhớ tiến trình một lần.
  • XOR: single-byte XOR has only 256 possible keys — brute force, or XOR a known plaintext (a PE "MZ" header) against the blob to derive the key. A hard-coded key + known algorithm/length lets you decrypt the blob directly.XOR một byte chỉ có 256 khóa khả dĩ — brute force, hoặc XOR một plaintext đã biết (header "MZ" của PE) với khối để suy khóa. Khóa hard-code + biết thuật toán/độ dài cho phép giải mã khối trực tiếp.
  • Implementation weaknesses:Điểm yếu hiện thực: strong algorithms don't help if the key is embedded/recoverable — extract it and decrypt captured C2. IV/nonce reuse leaks plaintext relationships; ECB shows repeating 16-byte ciphertext blocks for repeating plaintext; a key derived from a fixed string is identical on every infection (no real secrecy). Authors roll their own buggy crypto to stay small/evade signatures, often introducing exploitable flaws.thuật toán mạnh vô nghĩa nếu khóa được nhúng/khôi phục được — trích nó và giải mã C2 đã bắt. Tái dùng IV/nonce làm lộ quan hệ plaintext; ECB cho khối ciphertext 16 byte lặp khi plaintext lặp; khóa dẫn xuất từ chuỗi cố định là giống hệt ở mọi lần lây nhiễm (không bí mật thật). Tác giả tự viết crypto đầy lỗi để nhỏ gọn/né signature, thường tạo lỗ hổng khai thác được.

F8 — Windows Executable File Formats

  • PE / EXE / COM basics: DOS header, PE header, optional header, section table, imports, exports, resources, relocations.Cơ bản PE / EXE / COM: DOS header, PE header, optional header, bảng section, import, export, tài nguyên, relocation.
  • Useful fields: compile timestamp, subsystem, entry point, image base, section names/sizes/entropy, imports, digital signature.Trường hữu ích: timestamp biên dịch, subsystem, entry point, image base, tên / kích thước / entropy section, import, chữ ký số.
  • Suspicious signs: packed sections, RWX permissions, few imports, overlay data, invalid signature, strange entry point.Dấu hiệu đáng ngờ: section đã đóng gói, quyền RWX, ít import, dữ liệu overlay, chữ ký không hợp lệ, entry point lạ.
  • Extract value: strings, resources, embedded config, certificate info, icons, URLs, mutexes, import APIs.Trích xuất giá trị: chuỗi, tài nguyên, config nhúng, thông tin chứng chỉ, icon, URL, mutex, API import.

Packed vs Unpacked Indicators

IndicatorPackedUnpacked / Legitimate
EntropyHigh (>7.0) in code sections — compressed / encrypted dataLower (5.0-6.5) — readable code and data
Import tableFew imports; often only LoadLibrary + GetProcAddressRich import table with many specific API functions
Section namesUPX0, UPX1, .aspack, .themida, .vmp0, or random names.text, .data, .rdata, .rsrc — standard names
Section sizesSmall original section + large appended sectionProportional section sizes
Entry pointNot at .text start; may be in unusual sectionAt .text section or standard entry point
Overlay dataLarge data appended after PE sectionsMinimal or expected overlay

.NET Assemblies and PowerShell Obfuscation

  • .NET PE: Contains CLR metadata (type definitions, method names, strings) even when obfuscated; parse with dnSpy, ILSpy, or CFF Explorer.
  • .NET indicators: mscoree.dll import, CLR header in PE optional header, .NET-specific sections (.text with CLR structures).
  • PowerShell obfuscation techniques:
TechniqueExampleDetection
String concatenation"Pow"+"er"+"Shell"Reassemble strings; look for Invoke-Expression patterns.
Base64 encoding-EncodedCommand flagDecode Base64 payload; Event 4104 script block logging captures decoded commands.
Character replacement'IEX' -replace 'X','x'+'ec'Pattern matching for -replace, -join, -f format operator.
Tick insertionIn`vo`ke-Ex`pres`sionRemove backticks and match against known cmdlet names.
Environment variable expansion$env:ComSpec[4,15,25]-join''Expand environment variables and reconstruct strings.
Type casting[char]101+[char]120Evaluate character codes to reconstruct strings.
Exam tip: PowerShell Script Block Logging (Event 4104) captures the actual commands executed, even after deobfuscation. This is the most reliable source for PowerShell-based attacks.PowerShell Script Block Logging (Event 4104) bắt các lệnh thực sự được thực thi, ngay cả sau khi deobfuscation. Đây là nguồn đáng tin cậy nhất cho các cuộc tấn công dựa trên PowerShell.

PE Magic Bytes and Key Analysis Techniques

  • Magic bytes: PE files start with 4D 5A ("MZ") at byte 0. PE signature 50 45 00 00 ("PE\0\0") at offset in e_lfanew (byte 0x3C). These definitively identify PE files.
  • Import table (IAT) capability map:
    • CreateRemoteThread + WriteProcessMemory → process injection
    • CryptEncrypt / CryptGenKey → cryptographic operations (ransomware, C2)
    • WinExec / ShellExecute → code execution
    • RegSetValueEx → registry persistence
    • InternetConnect / HttpSendRequest → HTTP C2
  • Section entropy: Normal code ~6.0 bits / byte. Packed or encrypted section >7.0 bits = compressed / encrypted — unpack before further analysis.
  • CAPA tool: capa malware.exe → automatically maps binary capabilities to MITRE ATT&CK framework. Best triage tool for rapid capability identification.

Windows Executable (PE) File FormatĐịnh dạng file thực thi Windows (PE)

  • Identification:Nhận diện: every PE begins with the DOS "MZ" header; e_lfanew points to the "PE\0\0" signature. Identify by these magic bytes, not the extension (ZIP="PK", PDF="%PDF", ELF="\x7FELF"). Legacy .COM = flat binary (no PE header).mọi PE bắt đầu bằng header DOS "MZ"; e_lfanew trỏ tới chữ ký "PE\0\0". Nhận diện bằng magic byte này, không theo đuôi (ZIP="PK", PDF="%PDF", ELF="\x7FELF"). .COM cũ = binary phẳng (không có header PE).
  • Sections: .text (code), .data/.rdata (data, imports), .rsrc (resources), .reloc. IAT (imports) reveals capability; the loader fills it with resolved function addresses — IAT hooking swaps these pointers. EAT (exports) is relevant for DLLs. AddressOfEntryPoint = where execution starts (OEP after unpacking). TLS callbacks run before the entry point — used for anti-debug.(import) lộ năng lực; loader điền nó bằng địa chỉ hàm đã phân giải — IAT hooking đổi các con trỏ này. EAT (export) liên quan tới DLL. AddressOfEntryPoint = nơi thực thi bắt đầu (OEP sau khi giải nén). TLS callback chạy trước entry point — dùng cho anti-debug.
  • Packing indicators:Dấu hiệu pack: UPX-named sections, a W+X high-entropy section, or virtual size ≫ raw size (space for unpacked code). An overlay (data after the last section) often hides config/payload. The Rich header fingerprints the build toolchain; TimeDateStamp is forgeable. A PE importing mscoree.dll = .NET assembly (use dnSpy/ILSpy). strings is a fast first-pass triage.section tên UPX, một section W+X entropy cao, hoặc virtual size ≫ raw size (chỗ cho code giải nén). Một overlay (dữ liệu sau section cuối) thường giấu config/payload. Rich header fingerprint toolchain biên dịch; TimeDateStamp có thể giả. PE import mscoree.dll = assembly .NET (dùng dnSpy/ILSpy). strings là bước triage đầu nhanh.

F9 — Hiding Techniques

  • Process injection: CreateRemoteThread, WriteProcessMemory, process hollowing, reflective DLL injection, APC injection.Tiêm tiến trình: CreateRemoteThread, WriteProcessMemory, process hollowing, tiêm DLL phản chiếu, tiêm APC.
  • Rootkit hiding: SSDT patching, filter drivers, DKOM / process list manipulation, file / registry hiding.Ẩn náu rootkit: vá SSDT, driver filter, DKOM / thao túng danh sách tiến trình, ẩn file / registry.
  • Detection: memory malfind-style checks, suspicious RWX regions, hidden drivers, discrepancies between API and raw enumeration.Phát hiện: kiểm tra kiểu malfind trong bộ nhớ, vùng RWX đáng ngờ, driver ẩn, sự không khớp giữa API và liệt kê thô.
  • Hiding often overlaps with defence evasion and persistence.Ẩn náu thường chồng chéo với trốn tránh phòng thủ và persistence.

Common Techniques for Process Injection

TechniqueAPI SequenceMemory Indicator
Classic DLL InjectionOpenProcess → VirtualAllocEx → WriteProcessMemory → CreateRemoteThread(LoadLibrary)Unexpected DLL at unusual path in dlllist
Process HollowingCreateProcess(suspended) → UnmapViewOfSection → VirtualAllocEx → WriteProcessMemory → SetThreadContext → ResumeThreadLegit process name, wrong image base in malfind
Reflective DLL InjectionCustom loader in DLL maps itself from memory — no LoadLibrary callMZ / PE header in heap / stack, absent from module list
Thread HijackingSuspendThread → GetThreadContext → modify EIP / RIP → SetThreadContext → ResumeThreadRWX memory region not backed by file on disk
APC InjectionQueueUserAPC into alertable thread of target processUnusual write to target process memory + APC queue

SSDT Patching

SSDT = System Service Descriptor Table

  → Maps syscall numbers to kernel function addresses



Rootkit patches SSDT entries to point to malicious functions:

  Original: NtQuerySystemInformation → ntoskrnl!NtQuerySystemInformation

  Patched:  NtQuerySystemInformation → rootkit!FakeNtQuerySystemInformation



Effect: When userspace calls NtQuerySystemInformation (used by Task Manager,

        Process Explorer), the rootkit filters out its own process from results.



Detection: Compare live SSDT values against ntoskrnl.exe export addresses

           Tools: GMER, Volatility ssdt plugin, Windbg !chkimg

DKOM — Direct Kernel Object Manipulation

EPROCESS structures form a doubly-linked list (ActiveProcessLinks)

  Rootkit unlinks target EPROCESS from the list:

  

  Normal:   ProcessA ←→ ProcessB ←→ ProcessC

  After:    ProcessA ←→ ProcessC  (ProcessB unlinked but still in memory)



Effect: Tools using PsGetNextProcess (pslist) won't see ProcessB

        Tools scanning raw memory (psscan) WILL find ProcessB



Detection: psscan vs pslist discrepancy = DKOM indicator

Filter Driver Hiding

  • Malicious filter driver registered in I / O stack (filesystem, network, keyboard)
  • Intercepts IRP (I / O Request Packets) and modifies results
  • Can hide files (filesystem filter), network connections (TDI / NDIS filter), keystrokes (keyboard filter)
  • Detection: enumerate loaded drivers via Volatility modules / driverscan, check against signed driver list

Hiding Techniques — Injection & HookingKỹ thuật ẩn giấu — Tiêm & Hook

All process injection variants aim to run malicious code inside a legitimate process — evading detection and inheriting its trust/permissions.Mọi biến thể process injection nhằm chạy mã độc bên trong một tiến trình hợp lệ — né phát hiện và thừa hưởng tin cậy/quyền của nó.

TechniqueKỹ thuậtHowCách thức
CreateRemoteThreadOpenProcess → VirtualAllocEx → WriteProcessMemory → CreateRemoteThread
Process hollowingProcess hollowingStart a legit process suspended, unmap its image, write malicious code, resumeKhởi chạy tiến trình hợp lệ suspended, gỡ ánh xạ image, ghi mã độc, resume
Reflective DLLReflective DLLMap & run a DLL from memory (no disk file, not in module list)Ánh xạ & chạy DLL từ bộ nhớ (không có file đĩa, không trong danh sách module)
APC / thread hijackingQueueUserAPC, or SuspendThread + SetThreadContext + resume
  • Hooking: IAT hooking swaps import-table pointers; inline (detour) overwrites a function's first bytes with a jump to attacker code (works even for dynamically resolved calls).IAT hooking đổi con trỏ bảng import; inline (detour) ghi đè các byte đầu của hàm bằng lệnh nhảy tới mã của kẻ tấn công (chạy cả với lời gọi phân giải động).
  • Kernel hiding:Ẩn ở kernel: SSDT patching reroutes system calls through attacker code; DKOM unlinks the EPROCESS from the active-process list (psscan defeats it); filter drivers alter file-system I/O responses. DLL search-order hijacking / side-loading places a malicious DLL where Windows finds it before the legitimate one (or a signed EXE loads a malicious DLL from its folder).Vá SSDT chuyển hướng system call qua mã của kẻ tấn công; DKOM gỡ EPROCESS khỏi danh sách tiến trình hoạt động (psscan vô hiệu nó); filter driver sửa phản hồi I/O hệ thống tệp. DLL search-order hijacking / side-loading đặt DLL độc hại nơi Windows tìm trước DLL hợp lệ (hoặc EXE đã ký nạp DLL độc hại từ thư mục của nó).

F12 — Behavioural Analysis

  • Use tools to identify behaviour: Procmon, Process Hacker, Autoruns, Regshot, Wireshark, Fiddler, INetSim, sandbox reports.Dùng công cụ để xác định hành vi: Procmon, Process Hacker, Autoruns, Regshot, Wireshark, Fiddler, INetSim, báo cáo sandbox.
  • Observe file, registry, process, service, scheduled task, mutex, network, and persistence activity.Quan sát hoạt động file, registry, tiến trình, dịch vụ, scheduled task, mutex, mạng và persistence.
  • C2 aspects: destination, URI, user-agent, beacon interval, encryption / encoding, DNS pattern, fallback domains.Khía cạnh C2: đích, URI, user-agent, khoảng beacon, mã hóa / encoding, mẫu DNS, domain dự phòng.
  • Infection vectors and persistence mechanisms must be tied to evidence, not guessed.Vector lây nhiễm và cơ chế persistence phải được gắn với bằng chứng, không được đoán.
  • Sandbox limitations: malware may detect VM, require user interaction, time delay, internet, domain join, or specific locale.Giới hạn sandbox: malware có thể phát hiện VM, yêu cầu tương tác người dùng, trì hoãn thời gian, internet, tham gia domain hoặc locale cụ thể.

Sandbox Analysis Framework

File System Activity

Files created/modified/deleted. Temp directory drops. Executable dropped to unusual location (AppData, ProgramData, Windows\Temp).

Registry Activity

Run key writes (persistence). HKLM vs HKCU scope. Service creation. Config data written to unusual keys.

Network Activity

DNS resolution of hardcoded domains. HTTP / S connections with unusual user-agent. Beaconing interval. Data volume outbound.

Process Activity

Child process spawned. Injection into other process. Self-deletion. Anti-analysis checks (IsDebuggerPresent, VM detection).

✓
Tools Summary — Malware Analysis

Static: PE-bear, CFF Explorer, CAPA, DIE (Detect-It-Easy), FLOSS (string extraction), Ghidra / IDA.

Dynamic: x64dbg, WinDbg, Process Monitor, Process Hacker, Wireshark, FakeNet-NG, Any.run, Cuckoo Sandbox.

Memory: Volatility3, Rekall.

Dynamic Analysis Workflow

  1. Setup environment: Isolated VM (no internet), snapshot before execution, INetSim or FakeNet-NG to simulate network.
  2. Before-state capture: Regshot snapshot 1, Procmon start, Wireshark start.
  3. Execute malware: Run sample, wait sufficient time (VM sandbox evasion may need >5 minutes).
  4. After-state capture: Regshot snapshot 2 → compare, Procmon save log, Wireshark save pcap.
  5. Analyze outputs:
    • File activity: files dropped/modified/deleted, especially %TEMP%, %APPDATA%, Startup.
    • Registry: persistence keys (Run, RunOnce, Services, Winlogon).
    • Network: C2 destinations, beacon interval, DNS queries, User-Agent.
    • Process: parent-child spawning, injected processes, mutexes.

Sandbox Evasion Indicators

  • Malware checks CPUID (VM bit), number of CPU cores (<2 = sandbox), uptime (<10 minutes = sandbox).Malware kiểm tra CPUID (bit VM), số lõi CPU (<2 = sandbox), thời gian hoạt động (<10 phút = sandbox).
  • Checks number of files on Desktop, screen resolution, specific VMware / VirtualBox registry keys.Kiểm tra số file trên Desktop, độ phân giải màn hình, khóa registry VMware / VirtualBox cụ thể.
  • Delays execution beyond typical sandbox observation windows (>5 minutes).Trì hoãn thực thi vượt qua cửa sổ quan sát sandbox điển hình (>5 phút).
  • Requires user interaction (mouse movement, clicks) or specific environment (domain join, locale).Yêu cầu tương tác người dùng (di chuyển chuột, click) hoặc môi trường cụ thể (tham gia domain, locale).

Behavioural (Dynamic) AnalysisPhân tích hành vi (động)

  • Precondition:Điều kiện tiên quyết: always detonate in an isolated lab (VM/network with no path to production/the internet); take a snapshot before each run to revert to a clean baseline and stay reproducible.luôn kích nổ trong lab cách ly (VM/mạng không có đường tới production/internet); chụp snapshot trước mỗi lần để khôi phục về baseline sạch và tái lập được.
  • Toolset: Procmon (file/registry/process events) + Wireshark + INetSim/FakeNet (simulate internet so the sample reveals C2 without real exposure) + Regshot (before/after state diff) give the fullest behavioural picture. Observing a created service/Run key identifies persistence; named pipes (e.g. \\.\pipe\msagent_xx) can be host IoCs.Procmon (sự kiện file/registry/tiến trình) + Wireshark + INetSim/FakeNet (giả lập internet để mẫu lộ C2 mà không phơi nhiễm thật) + Regshot (so sánh trạng thái trước/sau) cho bức tranh hành vi đầy đủ nhất. Quan sát service/khóa Run được tạo xác định persistence; named pipe (vd \\.\pipe\msagent_xx) có thể là IoC host.
  • Anti-analysis:Chống phân tích: samples evade sandboxes via VM checks (MAC OUIs, registry, low core count), time delays / mouse-movement checks, and anti-debugging (IsDebuggerPresent, PEB BeingDebugged, rdtsc timing). Counter with hardened VMs, longer run times, sleep-patching and stealth debuggers.mẫu né sandbox qua kiểm tra VM (OUI MAC, registry, ít nhân), trì hoãn thời gian / kiểm tra chuyển động chuột, và anti-debug (IsDebuggerPresent, PEB BeingDebugged, đo thời gian rdtsc). Đối phó bằng VM gia cố, thời gian chạy lâu hơn, vá sleep và debugger ẩn.
  • Strengths & limits:Điểm mạnh & giới hạn: dynamic analysis forces packed samples to unpack and reveal real behaviour, but only shows code that executes in that session — logic gated behind dates/commands/conditions stays dormant. Combine with static reversing for full coverage.phân tích động buộc mẫu đã pack tự giải nén và lộ hành vi thật, nhưng chỉ cho thấy mã thực thi trong phiên đó — logic phụ thuộc ngày/lệnh/điều kiện sẽ nằm im. Kết hợp với dịch ngược tĩnh để phủ đầy đủ.

ACPO / NPCC 4 Principles

  1. Do not alter data that may be relied upon in court.
  2. If original data must be accessed, the person must be competent and able to explain actions.
  3. Maintain an audit trail sufficient for an independent examiner to reproduce the result.
  4. The case officer has overall responsibility for compliance with law and principles.

1Reconnaissance8Credential Access
2Resource Development9Discovery

Exam Traps to Memorise

#TrapCorrect exam logic
1Volatile evidence firstCollect RAM, network state, running processes, and open handles before disk-only artefacts when safe and within scope.
2Do not alter evidence unnecessarilyDo not delete malware, reboot, power off, or change systems unless justified for containment and fully documented.
3Legal / client escalationConsult client / legal before involving third parties, except mandatory escalation cases such as CSAM or explicit legal duty.
4Read inverted MCQ wordingWatch for NOT, LEAST, EXCEPT, FIRST, BEST, and MOST APPROPRIATE. CPIA often tests judgement order.
5Base64Encoding, not encryption. No key is required. Decode first, then analyse the decoded content.
6HashingHashes are one-way fingerprints, not encryption. You compare, verify, or crack / brute-force hashes; you do not decrypt them.
7MD5 vs SHA-256MD5 is weak for strong integrity assurance but still useful for quick triage and tool compatibility. Prefer SHA-256 for evidence integrity.
8IoC strengthHash / IP / domain IoCs are precise but fragile. Behavioural IoCs and TTPs are more resilient but require context.
9IP address attributionIP address alone is not identity. Correlate DHCP lease time, NAT translation, hostname, user logon, and endpoint activity.
10Windows Logon TypesRDP is Logon Type 10. SMB / network access is Type 3. Local console login is Type 2. Do not confuse them.
11IDS alertAn IDS alert is not proof of compromise. Validate payload, direction, vulnerable target, context, and follow-on activity.
12VirusTotalVirusTotal is context, not proof. 0 detections does not mean safe; client consent is required before uploading sensitive samples.
13Prefetch vs ShimcachePrefetch is stronger evidence of execution. Shimcache / Amcache support presence / execution context but should be corroborated.
14pslist vs psscanpslist walks active process lists. psscan scans memory pools and may reveal hidden or terminated processes.
15MACB timestampsModified = content change. Changed = NTFS metadata change. Born later than Modified is suspicious, not absolute proof; corroborate with other artefacts.
